My main trading time frames are H4 & H1. I might use the minimum indicators for confluence sake, such as Trend Lines, Channels and sometimes Oscillators for divergence signals. The core of my trading technique is tracing the order flow and trading Price Action at decision areas. Based on this equation Volatility = High Volume + Low Liquidity , I will be using some terms that might have the same meaning as follows: Base = Cluster = Equilibrium = Indecision = Balance = Stable = Normal = Efficient = Range = Consolidation = Flag Decision = Imbalance = Unstable = Abnormal = Inefficient = Momentum = Impulse = Liquidity Gap/Spike = Engulf = Pole Corrective Moves = Rebalancing Level = Zone = Area Decision Point = Supply / Demand Pivot Zone = S/R Flip/Swap = Key Level Broken = Ignored False Breakout = Fakeout = Stop Run = Stop Hunt = Trap Positioning / Accumulation = Liquidation / Distribution You can find my following articles in the Markepedia , and I recommend reading all of the other great articles in there: 1). Order Flow 2). Trend Direction / Highs & Lows 3). Key Levels and Price Action There 4). Stacked Orders/Zones 5). Momentum Away / Liquidity Gap 6). Fakeouts All comments and critics are most welcomed. Welcome to my journal! Edit: I was one of the co-founders of RTM and it was fun for quite sometime but good things never last. I've decided to seperate myself from RTM and now I have my own place where I call home. This journal will remain here for the old time's sake. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 16 Jul 2012 19:48 #6 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 This short trade follows the previous long. had a DD but this time I didn't set a SL, so I waited for price at higher supply zone. However, my 2nd entry as it shown in last chart wasn't a right decision. Explaining the trapped traders... On the right side of attached gold chart there is a marked demand zone, this zone was a very clear and strong demand twice. One time to leave the zone at ( A ), another one as retest as a spike at ( B ). What do you think traders did when it came back at ( C )? They bought it. Well it happens that it was not the good thing to do here so they got trapped at ( C ). When price came back near their order price at ( D ) they could get out of their longs hence mechanically becoming sellers. Done..closed them all... Another example of trapped traders and price behavior there... Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Exporters & importers are a commercial traders and they hedge their international business transactions to maintain their planned profit margin in their base currency. So, the prices mentioned in the headline will effect their decision, but which are the majority in Australia.....exporters or importers ! You need an economist to research this. However I don't care about all of that, all I need is to monitor the two most significant levels in the COT chart to support my technical analysis. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 19 Mar 2013 20:11 #248 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Cable; there is nothing interestig on LTF so by looking at daily we see a bullish move created a LQ gap preceeded by a failed inverted pinbar which is a sign of trapped sellers and an area where price tends to bounce from. On the upper side there is compression (multiple FTRs) and a flag limit where price might create a fakeout. H4 and what to expect on it; price could drop from the current range to the trapped sellers area where we can buy targeting the higher levels, or price could rise to the fakeout area where we can sell targeting the lower level. Any significant price action other than what has been planned here could change the whole plan. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 13 Apr 2013 02:51 #261 sunnytrader1984 --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 375 Thank you received: 731 FXSniffer, May I? Romant, Bullish engulfing candle happening at a right place (in demand) would make a lot of sense, at the wrong place (in supply) would also make a lot of sense (non). Now depends which sense you choose. Here is for you, take the place where you marked you went long and just look 1 cm above, do you see a strong DAILY supply? For most of the traders here on RTM, this would have said already, DONT BUY, DONT BUY, DONT BUY! But you bought, now why did you buy.....because they made you buy. A strong bullish looking candle right into the supply serves well to catch retail longs for them to put their shorts in. This is a simple sentence, the day it makes sense for you.....you will be on your way. Here is one small suggestion, I wont call advice, coz I am not good enough. Forget about engulfing candles....why? because an engulfing candle on H4 might just be a lot of compression on M15. Here is what you need to start looking for....ZONE ENGULFS, DP ENGULFS, POINTY PLACE ENGULFS...dont get scared now, these are all the same things, we call them differently. I can suggest you where to start....CAPTAIN P's trading journal. read his first post...take his charts and go through each of them thinking for yourself, why he did what he did. There are goldmines here on many journals, you need to find your niche. Everybody has a different trading style, make your own! Good luck on this beautiful journey! Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 13 Apr 2013 06:24 #266 Infest --> Offline Associate Trader Reach perfection. Posts: 230 Thank you received: 412 Very important lesson for lots of traders, even the expierenced one's. Gold is in a HTF down trend so what we want is to enter shorts as long as price prints lower lows. And this fact occured just a few days ago on April 4th. Accomplished by big momentum down candles, so in no way we want to look for possible longs here, maybe scalp one's but in no case any long setups. We let the big boys decide to print higher highs on HTF so that we can enter at the retest with the lowest risk, but trying to get the turning point just doesn't make lots of sense. I'm also in this progress which gave me lots of "Aha-moments" and some very easy entries with ZERO risk and ZERO drawdown - because you go with the trend. So all you have to do is find where the big decisions were made from the big boys. FX Sniffers last post contains all the information. Especially his first picture. "Engulfed previous low - engulfed previous low - engulfed previous low"... this should always be the first thing that comes in mind when looking for trading decisions. And on daily the level at 1589 (support line) that got engulfed by big momentum candles AND engulfing previous low makes up a more than perfect entry. I do not know how many traders took this trade at the 1589-level, but I am quite sure that many many of them just earned their money not even for this month, but also for the next - if they held it like FX Sniffer did. Yo have to pick FTRs carefully, not every time price fails to return is potential FTR. You have to combine your whole knowledge into it. In technical analysis you have to think of price as a machine that has gears in different sizes, the bigger gear needs power and strength to make one rotation and the smaller gears needs less power and strength, and in order to generate enough momentum to make the bigger gear rotate the smaller gears has to rotate multiple rotations in order for the bigger gear to make one rotation. Cycles in cycles this is how price moves. rexed9999 wrote: 4) Why do you say [8] is a minor demand? It looks like price moved very strongly from [8] and engulfed supply at [6]? On this TF the source of the rally is the original demand and anything alese would be minor or DP. however, [8] was a retest of broken minor supply on the left making it a DP but not a major demand. once again don't confuse yourself with my terminology, what matters is for you to understand the price characteristics and what does it react to and how. rexed9999 wrote: It won't let me upload a picture for some reason, says the file is too big even though it's only 196Kb, here is a link to the edited chart - min.us/llE860zFFIy6E I'm really sorry to have to bombard you with so many questions No worries glad to help. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 21 Apr 2013 17:30 #294 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Got a PM that better be answered in public... The message was saying that in our early days of traditional S/D trading we used to look for candle engulfing pattern and on RTM here almost nobody uses it but the engulf of the zone is in play. So, I have a question: why don't you use the engulfing candle pattern and why do you treat the zone engulfing as more reliable? My answer is: I still look at candle engulf when I look for S/D clusters and I look at other candle patterns as well, but the engulfing of areas is more reliable because "Price Dynamics" is not just a simple candle pattern but "a study of price in motion in regards to the supply & demand order flow", in other words; "the history of price action/reaction and behavior in relation to the supply & demand". Moreover, the candle engulf on HTF could be an engulf of a significant area on LTF, the areas could be decision points or highs and lows which are areas of significant interest and engulfing them means price wants to travel beyond that area, as well as bears or bulls on the other side are starting to weaken. What if you’re looking at the line chart and you don’t have any candles?! Think of candle engulfing as the basics of S/D, and the area engulfing as the advanced S/D. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 25 Apr 2013 19:35 #307 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 3D VS. 3T There is a difference between the 3 Drives and the 3 Touch aka 3 Taps, both are a result of distribution and accumulation. 3D falls under the compression category and is a reversal move. 3T falls under the trend following category and is a continuation move, but also could a compression when price returns to it and breaks the 3rd touch. The below image explain the difference and the 3D has been explained by IF here . As for the 3T its simply a 3 touch of a trend-line. In below image and on the left side is an example of a 3T into a down trend-line and the third touch was preceded by a lower low and formed a lower high, on the right side a 3T into an up trend-line and the third touch was preceded by a higher high then formed a higher low. In IF's example you can see both patterns in his chart, a 3T followed by a 3D. For more on trend direction and the role of highs and lows refer to the Trend Direction / Highs and Lows Article . I was considering 40% annual a good return. Now when my friend and I see your returns (yours, and others so on...) it's hard to believe this is possible. My friend looks at statistics and says that none of you guys are in Forbes 500 but with this % return all of you should be in there. Also claims that none of world best traders could average over 100% yearly. Well you get the point. Now my friend has the point I agree, but also I spend days here and I see what you do and how. Well to make this shorter could you maybe shed some light for me?” Now my feedback, I know why I’m not in Forbes. However, I had the same question regarding other traders (not necessarily in RTM) who shows their amazing consistent winners and makes you think that it’s so easy, and the issue that they don’t explain the reasons behind their entries and they never show you the bad trades. Some might learn something from their charts and others might not but in the end they could mislead you into a disaster. The internet is a double edged sword, anyone can post anything wether its false charts or demo results. Here in RTM we tried to avoid this by digging deep into the price action and teaching the reasons behind our entries and what is expected next, and we wish from whoever learns from our posts to carry on the torch and pass it over to new members. So if you want my advice whatever results you see on the internet by others doesn’t mean anything, all you need is a method or to learn how to read the price and your results depends on you and your circumstances, if someone is showing unbelievable results it’s not necessarily fake or even true, and it doesn’t mean that you could achieve the same because by end of the day it all comes back to a certain factors. 1) Your personality & your circumstances: in short you can call it “psychology”, human behavior is common and the proof is if you meet a group of traders and share your trading stories you’ll find that you all share a lot in common, the same mistakes, fears, greed etc. and only the elite who succeed in this business are the ones who succeed in controlling their feelings and bend the circumstances to their advantage. Each trader has different circumstances that could either help or add pressure, for example are you trading part time or full time and do you have enough savings or do you need every cent you can make from the market, does your family/GF/BF supports you or not and so on. 2) Your startup capital: the amount of money in your balance plays a big role, the bigger accounts the better, of course rules are made to be broken and a trader can make thousands out of a hundred but also there are traders who wasted millions. 3) Your money/risk management: this one is much related to 1 & 2 as a cause & effect. MM could vary depending on capital and the personality, bigger capital allows you to use bigger contracts and bad MM could demolish big accounts, solid MM ease psychological pressure and bad psychology destroy all MM rules, the examples are endless. 4) Market state or condition: in some market conditions you can make huge profits, in others you can’t make a clear decision. For example if we consider you’re implementing a solid MM plan trading one instrument and everything is perfect the monthly ROI will never ever have the same result for the 12 month. In fact all the above mentioned points are related and connected to each other as affects & effects. So back to the Forbes list, I’ve never posted consistent profitable trades neither here nor anywhere else and never claimed that I achieve a certain monthly ROI, so this question is irrelevant to me but it could be asked to others if they claim or show such results. As for the yearly return % in general it depends on all the above points. In some of the live competitions traders have achieved more than a 100% ROI in one month, as for institutions, banks, hedge funds, regulated managed accounts etc their risk profiles are monitored & controlled by regulations so as their returns. Moreover, not all private independent traders show off or publish their ROI. Therefore, if you find anyone on the internet or anywhere else publishing their results without teaching then either they are scammers or showing off or looking for investors or selling something. Here in RTM we had an objective to teach and maybe someday become a physical academy or even startup a fund management, this is why most of our posts are showing the why and how before or after and not just after the facts results. So in short take the trading methods as it is or tweak it and ignore others results, don’t think about it, in the end your returns depends on YOU. I don’t guarantee that you’ll make a 100% yearly ROI or less or more because I don’t know you or your circumstances but I guarantee that you’ll save a lot of time in your journey. Reason: I missed the QM Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 14 Sep 2013 07:20 #321 Hotochin --> Offline Trader Posts: 588 Thank you received: 729 The first is the daily chart. The only main point here is to see the very important level that price was coming into. Price generally either flagged above or below this line. The second chart is the 30min. Basically you have price come into the green line and FO by the pin bar. As Pedini has said the FO really fuels the move in the opposite direction. After the FO price engulfs the green ellipse and gives clues that it wants to go up farther. Your entry is by the return to the source of the engulf marked with a purple line. The last chart is your 1min. I would bet that you went long when price returned to the green rectangle as price had already gone up and taken out supply. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 19 Dec 2013 21:26 #335 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Founders and members of RTM, I’ve been in this market since 2003 and during all these years I’ve never been an active forum member or blogger, until the PIE thread back at FF has changed that for some time all the way to AG until our journals were taken from us then Ifmyante gathered our group and we all helped in founding RTM. The idea of RTM was to create a global home for traders who share the same trading style based on a pure price action and we all have shared the same dream of developing this community to become a free school for new traders and in addition to a fee service to the elite or whoever want a special mentoring, as well as to start up a fund management service by creating our own capital corporation as you can see in the ladder page where I myself have created the organizational structure slides. We all had great ideas and each of us was unique with his/her own unique expertise and skills. However, speaking for myself the first few months spent in founding RTM and establishing the foundation of a trading group has caused me too much as a waste of time on something that requires a non-trader to work on. Since the end of 2011 and I’ve been a full-time trader and my life was great before RTM till it flipped over. Let me be clear here, I’m not blaming RTM for anything, actually I blame none but myself. However, I’ve learned a great lesson from that period of time and a true serious trader who depends a lot on his trading income shouldn’t be able to spend time on chatting, blogging, online communities, etc. unless in a certain special cases if the trader has a spare time, or has another source of income, or seeking a new source of income. Everyone in the cyber space has his/her own agenda and when agendas are gathered together they either meet or collide. My own agenda is to be the best I can be as a trader in regards to knowledge and experience (this has already been confirmed to me personally by the testimonial of an institutional trader and a market maker), and to make my first million out of trading which I failed to do this year. All I desire is to live on my profits from trading as much as I can squeeze out of it, no need to manage other’s money or to offer a commercial service because if I do so then I’m not the TRADER I’m seeking to be, I could be anything else like an analyst or a teacher or a scammer but not a TRADER. With my skills and a decent capital I can achieve what I desire so why should I waste time and efforts on anything else when I can spend it on family, friends, by the pool, or whatever!!! However, as mentioned before if I have the spare time and it’s not going to affect me in any bad way or cause me trouble then maybe I would give sometime to blogging or whatever even though that I’ve learned one should keep his knowledge to himself, but depending on a human nature some are too generous or like to give something back. My advice from an anonymous to every serious trader and it’s up to you to take it or leave it, seek the knowledge anywhere you can find it but don’t waste your time on anything more if you want to succeed as a trader. Trading requires a lot of mental awareness and sensibility so don’t let anything or anyone else disturb it. Stop wasting your time on child’s play and pay attention to the chart or read macro-economic or intermarket analysis or anything that will help you to develop and reach your extremes. Start reading between the lines and stop being spoon-fed. Don’t chase everything that is glowing in the dark or you could end up in a deep hole. Stop arguing about nonsense and let it be, take what works for you and create your own style, don’t mimic others and don’t let them affect you with their own styles, be unique and digest whatever you can, don’t try to absorb everything one-shot, take your time and step by step. Having said that, I’ve decided to remove myself from any association with any website/group which I don’t solely own or have a full control over, including RTM and by doing so I’ve published my own articles in my own blog, and I don’t know if I will ever post over there or do any new activities but for the time being my work should be in the right place where it belongs. Therefore, I ‘m requesting RTM to remove my articles from the markepedia and any other work I’ve done for RTM except for this journal which I expect to remain here for the old time’s sake. This shall be my last post here and any other public forum. Please don’t post any questions or send me any private messages which I’m not going to answer. Oh yeah, and I’m not asking anyone to follow me because I have nothing to offer and have no secret agenda. RTM admins, founders and members I wish you all the best and many thanks to all of you and please forgive me for my faults and mistakes. May you find peace. FAREWELL. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 08 Nov 2012 17:18 #82 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 The empty space surrounding the candles/bars is full of limit orders to buy and sell. Trades happens when these limits orders gets hit by market orders, thus reducing the number of limit orders at a level and this is what causes the market to move up and down. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. The following user(s) said Thank You: extremerrage , cuimingzhao Sniffing Snake Tracks... Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 08 Nov 2012 17:30 #83 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 I've posted this in another topic and thought its time to bring it here. Supply and Demand zones presents a stack of unfilled orders either offers or bids. Stacked zones presents stacked groups of unfilled orders (limit orders), in every zone there are unfilled stacked orders on top of each other. As Sam Seiden explained in a webinar - Unfilled orders moves market prices, the chart line/candles/bars represent filled/executed orders and the white space around it represent the unfilled orders (limit orders) thus we need to focus on the big unfilled orders (limit orders). What I'll explain here based on my understanding of the market, so is the attached chart. The following example applies to the stacked zones as well as to stacked orders inside a zone. In case of a supply zone scenario, offers shall be stacked above each other (the wider the zone the more orders at different prices) logically the majority would sell high and this is why unfilled orders would increase higher into the zone, and with every return of price to the zone the sell orders are decrease/consumed.....now this doesn't mean the smart or big money would be sitting at the highest prices....nope not necessarily....they are the smart money and they know where to set and distribute their offers...well, it could be among the highest offers and "for example" this is where a compression ends, or it might be among the lowest offers and this is when we see a spike or a quick touch of the zone's lower edge then price bounce and drops for a long distant before returning back and starts pressuring or forming ascending triangle to consumes the entire zone. Of course the strength of buyers and sellers plays a big role in this process every time it happens, thus comes the importance of how did the price leave the zone, and how is price returning to the zone. I hope I made it simple and clear. P.S. The attached chart present Buy/Sell limit orders. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. I don't know how the pros play it but for me its a sign that i respect on HTF and would enter the entrancement on LTF if its in confluence with levels. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 10 Jan 2013 21:15 #138 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Before I post again about the LQ gap I decided to give a brief about the mechanism of order flow. When we look at the market watch window in our trading platform we'll see instrument's symbols and next to it two different prices, one is bid and another is ask or offer. These prices are offered to us through an online broker who is not just a broker but also a dealer. Here is an explanation of the difference between a dealer and a broker from “Trading & Exchanges – By Larry Harris” “Dealers and brokers help people trade. Dealers trade with their clients when their clients want to trade. The prices at which a dealer will buy and sell are the dealer's bid and ask prices. After they trade with their clients, dealers then try to trade out at a profit by selling what they have bought or by buying back what they have sold. In effect, clients pay dealers to take their trading problems. The dealers then try to solve them at a profit. Dealers profit by buying low and selling high. Successful dealers must be excellent traders. Brokers are agents who arrange trades for their clients. They help their clients find traders who are willing to trade with them. They profit by charging commissions.” Dealers are market makers and also known as market specialists, so they play a big role as a market participant beside other pro money, understand how they play and you'll play with them. Now we understand that online brokers act as brokers and dealers. Back to the market watch window, the ask/offer shows the lowest offer price which the market is willing to sell at and for you to buy, the bid shows the highest bid price which the market is willing to buy at and for you to sell. I’ve already explained in posts # 82 & 83 that there are two types of orders “market orders” and “limit orders”, in the below image I’m showing an example of an order book aka; DOM, Price Ladder, Level II. Bear in mind this is only for explanation purposes, in the currency and metals market there is no depth of market, it only applies to centralized markets such as futures, equities and commodities. Of course banks and dealers have their own order books but it only shows their clients orders. Back to the image, the offers column for sell limit orders, bids for buy limit orders and in middle is the price. I’ve taken eur/usd prices as an example, the orders are standard lots/contracts (i.e. 200K = 2 lots and 1M = 10 lots), the two prices highlighted in yellow presents the Bid/Ask prices which we see on our market watch (spread in this example is unrealistic and only to make it easier). Anyone entering the market with market orders will be buying at 1.3010 and selling at 1.300. Now let’s say you place buy market orders of 500K (5 lots) what will happen?! Your orders will be executed at the lowest offer consuming the 200K at 1.3010 and a 300K out of 500K at 1.3020 and this price will be the new lowest offer with 200K limit orders, so as offers were consumed price moved up to 1.3020 Another scenario; you are placing a sell market orders of 1 million (10 lots) what is the outcome?! Your orders will be executed at the highest bid consuming the 400K at 1.3000, the 200K at 1.2980, the 300K at 1.2970 and a 100K out of 500K at 1.2950 and this will be the new highest bid with 400K limit orders, so as bids were consumed price moved down to 1.2950 This is how price moves up or down by consuming orders, and bigger orders generate buying/selling pressure (momentum) pushing price in its direction consuming smaller orders until it stops (slow down /stalls) at a new price where there are opposite bigger orders are placed. One last thing, the stop loss pending orders considered as limit orders and when it’s executed it adds pressure in the opposite direction. Hope it makes sense. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 10 Jan 2013 21:47 #139 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 benhur wrote: I hope it is alright to ask you to elaborate more about "Liquidity Gaps"? What is your definition for it, and how (and why) you expect of price reaction at this zone? There is no easy way to answer this question as it requires a study of the market microstructure which explains how the exchange occurs in financial markets and the order flow process, but I’ll try my best to make it simple. First of all, kindly refer to my first post here for some of the terms which in my opinion share the same meaning/definition. Second, take a look at my previous post. Now back to basics, we know that in a normal market there is a buyer for every seller and vice versa, this is considered a stable state of the market as it creates a balance between supply and demand and maintains a stable price value, and as the imbalance occurs in supply and demand the value of price increases or decreases. The balance state on chart is presented by a consolidation in price, and the imbalance is presented by a high volume/momentum candle (also known as a sign of Pro money / Institution’s buying or selling). If we zoom into the consolidation (LTF) we’ll find other states of balance and imbalance. In post # 90 I tried to explain the orders execution of Pro money but not in an accurate way for the sake of simplicity but I fixed this in post # 138 . So to recap, to have your orders executed at current price you need someone on the opposite side with the same number of orders otherwise price will move too much from your entry until it finds an opposite stack of orders. Retail traders have a big advantage over Pro money since their orders gets executed at current price, a benefit that Pro money doesn't have due to their mass orders which gets executed at different prices as the price moves away from their initial entry. Hence they tend to accumulate/distribute their orders through positioning, re-entering on retracements or even creating them as well as fakeouts. By now you understand why the sign of pro money buying/selling is presented by a high momentum candles aka liquidity gaps, but if you didn’t then continue reading. A liquidity gap means low liquidity! How?! You know that for every seller there is buyer and vice versa, but you also know that online brokers doesn't guarantee a non-slippage or a no requote during the time of volatile news due to low liquidity, and you can clearly see it on charts during such news when a long spike or a high momentum candle is created in seconds. A broker wouldn’t take the risk if you’re buying because they can't find you a seller unless they are willing to take the risk by selling to you as a dealer. So how could we say it’s a low liquidity when price moves in a high volume?! Again, we know that high volume candles are signs of big money, institutions etc. entering the market or in control of the market pushing price in their direction...that is true. However, the liquidity in this case is being injected from one side only (the dominant side) hence the name liquidity gap and the equation “Volatility = High Volume + Low Liquidity”. Now bear with me...The "normal or balanced state" of the market is to have a seller for every buyer thus maintaining an efficient change in price value, so when the market moves up or down either in consolidation or in a ladder steps behavior then it’s a “normal balanced state”, but if it moves up or down with a high volatility and momentum then its "abnormal or imbalanced state" and it’s a sign of domination from one side. So what is the conclusion?! The dominant side (pro money) would want to collect their profits as soon as price reaches their targets which results in market returning to its previous state (rebalancing) on a wider scale and filling up the liquidity gaps. I've answered the definition part in your question; now let’s answer the other part related to price reaction. I hope you were paying attention to the Fiber and Cable as I suggested in post # 134 and you can see the marked LQ gaps in the process of getting filled. Look at the attached Gold daily chart and see the price behavior when it broke through a decision area/major key level of significance, creating a high momentum candle as a sign of one side in control bulls or bears and how they wanted to push it higher or lower, of course price will return to fill the LQ gaps but later in the future, not at the present time of the incident specially not when it starts creating a new DP/Supply/Demand as pro money position their entries. On the other hand you can notice larger high momentum candles (LQ gaps) gets filled the same day or next day but this happens when price fails to break through a major DP or key level and this is when pro money are accumulating/distributing (bottom and top side of the chart). The market is in a constant state/phase shifting from normal to abnormal, balanced to imbalanced and back again, just the same as in ranging to trending and back again (refer to Wyckoff Market Theory), and every range contains the two phases if you zoom in, the same applies to the bottom consolidated area in chart. Supply & Demand trading style is in harmony with order flow and it works according to this stuff. IMO if we learn how the market works from the beginning it would save us a lot of time in our journey. Hope I made it clear and simple. On H4 we have our S/D zones marked, and the minor S/R flip from D1 is replaced by a decision point (minor demand zone) on this TF. Price created a new low when it bounced from the DP and it was coming from a bigger HH (top of the chart), then started creating a smaller High, HL followed by a HH, the last HL is ATF as we didn’t know that price will flip at that point before the bullish move. On H1 we have the same zones from H4 in addition to a new demand zone from H1, two new S/R flip from the same TF (yellow pointers showing the support/resistance flip areas), and a TL for guidance. We can clearly see price was flagging into H1 demand to fill up the LQ gaps on lift (in other words…pole > bullish flag pattern). The orange pointer #1 pointing at a breakout and retest of TL, and a “breakthrough close above” S/R flip, the small bearish candle with tail was sitting at the level and a sign of buying, next price moved up and again broke through the upper S/R flip and closed above it and made a nice retest and it can be clearly seen on LTFs. Next price shoot to the first H4 supply zone, ignored it and continued to second H4 supply. In the second H1 chart I’m showing a different scenario of price behavior if it dropped deeper into H1 demand zone, in this case it would have a been a selling pressure to consume demand. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 16 Jan 2013 23:12 #153 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 MCFly wrote: I'm not sure I like the explaination in the vids about why the gaps tend to get closed but in the end they mostly do. Everyone points at S/D, Balance and Imbalance in a different way without explaining the real mechanics behind it. Although in my previous posts I tried to make it clear why LQ gap gets filled but I’ll repeat it in a simple phrase; In LTF LQ gaps gets filled because - When pro money enter with their big orders it moves the price away and against their entry price, therefore they await for price to return to their entry price to continue adding their orders. In HTF LQ gaps get filled because – When pro money closes their buy orders they become sellers, and when they close their sell orders they become buyers. Thank you. Tony Robbins Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 05 Feb 2013 00:50 #183 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Reading COT (Commitment of Traders) charts to identify the long term market sentiment; COT charts are based on COT reports which are published by the CFTC each Friday on 19:30 GMT. From outsource: There are several reasons why COT charts provide valuable information about changes in the position commitments of traders: - Flips in the overall positioning may be an accurate trending indicator - Extreme positioning has historically been accurate in identifying FX market reversals - Changes in overall number of contracts can be used to determine strength of trend The below chart shows the recent Commercial Traders Net (Hedgers, Exporters, Importers), Large Speculators Net (hedge funds, banks etc) and Small Traders Net. What concern me are the Commercial and the Speculators, and the index values of 100 and Zero on the vertical axis. Knowing that when one is at 100 the other will be at Zero and vice versa. So, let’s take a look at the euro COT chart and compare it with eur/usd chart. Starting with old history from 5 Jun 2012, Com traders index was at 100 and Spec index at 0 and eur futures price 1.2446 (marked by a vertical dotted line on COT chart). Present time on 29 Jan 2013, Com traders index at 0 and Spec traders index at 100, and eur futures price 1.3665 In below chart prices are marked by horizontal lines and dates marked by vertical lines. The first lower crossing on the left side at [1] represents the old history and the second higher crossing on the right side at [2] represents the present. The weekly chart for an overall view and daily is for a zoom in view. Now, let’s start the analysis…..at [1] the Com were very long and Spec were very short, so what happened in price? It moved up above support level and into a decision area of supply followed by a move down to the 1.2446 value and bounced up retesting S/R level then back down into demand area from weekly TF where the stacked buy orders are, and price reversed in direction to rally up. Now open your eyes wide open and look at the level 1.2446 and see how it acted as an S/R flip level. So why price didn’t reverse directly from 1.2446?? Simply because of positioning. At [2] present time the Com are very short and Spec are very long, so what will happen? Currently we can see price bouncing down from 1.3665 to S/R level and two stacked decision areas of demand, but where are the stack sell orders on chart??! Yes at the higher decision area of stacked supply. So let’s see how it goes in the coming days. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. My main trading time frames are H4 & H1. I might use the minimum indicators for confluence sake, such as Trend Lines, Channels and sometimes Oscillators for divergence signals. The core of my trading technique is tracing the order flow and trading Price Action at decision areas. Based on this equation Volatility = High Volume + Low Liquidity , I will be using some terms that might have the same meaning as follows: Base = Cluster = Equilibrium = Indecision = Balance = Stable = Normal = Efficient = Range = Consolidation = Flag Decision = Imbalance = Unstable = Abnormal = Inefficient = Momentum = Impulse = Liquidity Gap/Spike = Engulf = Pole Corrective Moves = Rebalancing Level = Zone = Area Decision Point = Supply / Demand Pivot Zone = S/R Flip/Swap = Key Level Broken = Ignored False Breakout = Fakeout = Stop Run = Stop Hunt = Trap Positioning / Accumulation = Liquidation / Distribution You can find my following articles in the Markepedia , and I recommend reading all of the other great articles in there: 1). Order Flow 2). Trend Direction / Highs & Lows 3). Key Levels and Price Action There 4). Stacked Orders/Zones 5). Momentum Away / Liquidity Gap 6). Fakeouts All comments and critics are most welcomed. Welcome to my journal! Edit: I was one of the co-founders of RTM and it was fun for quite sometime but good things never last. I've decided to seperate myself from RTM and now I have my own place where I call home. This journal will remain here for the old time's sake. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 16 Jul 2012 19:48 #6 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 This short trade follows the previous long. had a DD but this time I didn't set a SL, so I waited for price at higher supply zone. However, my 2nd entry as it shown in last chart wasn't a right decision. Explaining the trapped traders... On the right side of attached gold chart there is a marked demand zone, this zone was a very clear and strong demand twice. One time to leave the zone at ( A ), another one as retest as a spike at ( B ). What do you think traders did when it came back at ( C )? They bought it. Well it happens that it was not the good thing to do here so they got trapped at ( C ). When price came back near their order price at ( D ) they could get out of their longs hence mechanically becoming sellers. Done..closed them all... Another example of trapped traders and price behavior there... Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 19 Mar 2013 20:11 #248 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Cable; there is nothing interestig on LTF so by looking at daily we see a bullish move created a LQ gap preceeded by a failed inverted pinbar which is a sign of trapped sellers and an area where price tends to bounce from. On the upper side there is compression (multiple FTRs) and a flag limit where price might create a fakeout. H4 and what to expect on it; price could drop from the current range to the trapped sellers area where we can buy targeting the higher levels, or price could rise to the fakeout area where we can sell targeting the lower level. Any significant price action other than what has been planned here could change the whole plan. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 13 Apr 2013 02:51 #261 sunnytrader1984 --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 375 Thank you received: 731 FXSniffer, May I? Romant, Bullish engulfing candle happening at a right place (in demand) would make a lot of sense, at the wrong place (in supply) would also make a lot of sense (non). Now depends which sense you choose. Here is for you, take the place where you marked you went long and just look 1 cm above, do you see a strong DAILY supply? For most of the traders here on RTM, this would have said already, DONT BUY, DONT BUY, DONT BUY! But you bought, now why did you buy.....because they made you buy. A strong bullish looking candle right into the supply serves well to catch retail longs for them to put their shorts in. This is a simple sentence, the day it makes sense for you.....you will be on your way. Here is one small suggestion, I wont call advice, coz I am not good enough. Forget about engulfing candles....why? because an engulfing candle on H4 might just be a lot of compression on M15. Here is what you need to start looking for....ZONE ENGULFS, DP ENGULFS, POINTY PLACE ENGULFS...dont get scared now, these are all the same things, we call them differently. I can suggest you where to start....CAPTAIN P's trading journal. read his first post...take his charts and go through each of them thinking for yourself, why he did what he did. There are goldmines here on many journals, you need to find your niche. Everybody has a different trading style, make your own! Good luck on this beautiful journey! Posts: 230 Thank you received: 412 Very important lesson for lots of traders, even the expierenced one's. Gold is in a HTF down trend so what we want is to enter shorts as long as price prints lower lows. And this fact occured just a few days ago on April 4th. Accomplished by big momentum down candles, so in no way we want to look for possible longs here, maybe scalp one's but in no case any long setups. We let the big boys decide to print higher highs on HTF so that we can enter at the retest with the lowest risk, but trying to get the turning point just doesn't make lots of sense. I'm also in this progress which gave me lots of "Aha-moments" and some very easy entries with ZERO risk and ZERO drawdown - because you go with the trend. So all you have to do is find where the big decisions were made from the big boys. FX Sniffers last post contains all the information. Especially his first picture. "Engulfed previous low - engulfed previous low - engulfed previous low"... this should always be the first thing that comes in mind when looking for trading decisions. And on daily the level at 1589 (support line) that got engulfed by big momentum candles AND engulfing previous low makes up a more than perfect entry. I do not know how many traders took this trade at the 1589-level, but I am quite sure that many many of them just earned their money not even for this month, but also for the next - if they held it like FX Sniffer did. If you're pointing at my chart then true as explained in my post, but if you're pointing at your chart then technically yes but it didn't go to [9] because of the S/R by the high at [6] and the low at [5]. Yo have to pick FTRs carefully, not every time price fails to return is potential FTR. You have to combine your whole knowledge into it. In technical analysis you have to think of price as a machine that has gears in different sizes, the bigger gear needs power and strength to make one rotation and the smaller gears needs less power and strength, and in order to generate enough momentum to make the bigger gear rotate the smaller gears has to rotate multiple rotations in order for the bigger gear to make one rotation. Cycles in cycles this is how price moves. rexed9999 wrote: 4) Why do you say [8] is a minor demand? It looks like price moved very strongly from [8] and engulfed supply at [6]? How do you qualify between a minor and major supply demand area i.e. what makes [8] different to [1]? On this TF the source of the rally is the original demand and anything alese would be minor or DP. however, [8] was a retest of broken minor supply on the left making it a DP but not a major demand. once again don't confuse yourself with my terminology, what matters is for you to understand the price characteristics and what does it react to and how. rexed9999 wrote: It won't let me upload a picture for some reason, says the file is too big even though it's only 196Kb, here is a link to the edited chart - min.us/llE860zFFIy6E I'm really sorry to have to bombard you with so many questions No worries glad to help. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 21 Apr 2013 17:30 #294 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Got a PM that better be answered in public... The message was saying that in our early days of traditional S/D trading we used to look for candle engulfing pattern and on RTM here almost nobody uses it but the engulf of the zone is in play. So, I have a question: why don't you use the engulfing candle pattern and why do you treat the zone engulfing as more reliable? My answer is: I still look at candle engulf when I look for S/D clusters and I look at other candle patterns as well, but the engulfing of areas is more reliable because "Price Dynamics" is not just a simple candle pattern but "a study of price in motion in regards to the supply & demand order flow", in other words; "the history of price action/reaction and behavior in relation to the supply & demand". Moreover, the candle engulf on HTF could be an engulf of a significant area on LTF, the areas could be decision points or highs and lows which are areas of significant interest and engulfing them means price wants to travel beyond that area, as well as bears or bulls on the other side are starting to weaken. What if you’re looking at the line chart and you don’t have any candles?! Think of candle engulfing as the basics of S/D, and the area engulfing as the advanced S/D. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 23 Apr 2013 16:07 #302 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Hi Hotochin, True.....conventional S/D trading suggests buying/selling from zone to zone at the first return back and if there are liquidity gaps on the left it will get filled in the process. However, a break through key levels or S/R and a close above or below helps to identify the shift in decision and the creation of a new decision point, and if this happens then the liquidity gaps will not get filled anytime soon and it's a sign of a strong trend depending on the time frame. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 25 Apr 2013 19:35 #307 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 3D VS. 3T There is a difference between the 3 Drives and the 3 Touch aka 3 Taps, both are a result of distribution and accumulation. 3D falls under the compression category and is a reversal move. 3T falls under the trend following category and is a continuation move, but also could a compression when price returns to it and breaks the 3rd touch. The below image explain the difference and the 3D has been explained by IF here . As for the 3T its simply a 3 touch of a trend-line. In below image and on the left side is an example of a 3T into a down trend-line and the third touch was preceded by a lower low and formed a lower high, on the right side a 3T into an up trend-line and the third touch was preceded by a higher high then formed a higher low. In IF's example you can see both patterns in his chart, a 3T followed by a 3D. For more on trend direction and the role of highs and lows refer to the Trend Direction / Highs and Lows Article . Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 13 Sep 2013 20:29 #315 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 I’ve received an important pm that better be answered here, here is a part of it: “Now before I met you guys here at RTM this year my view of what I can make in forex was different. I was considering 40% annual a good return. Now when my friend and I see your returns (yours, and others so on...) it's hard to believe this is possible. My friend looks at statistics and says that none of you guys are in Forbes 500 but with this % return all of you should be in there. Also claims that none of world best traders could average over 100% yearly. Well you get the point. Now my friend has the point I agree, but also I spend days here and I see what you do and how. Well to make this shorter could you maybe shed some light for me?” Now my feedback, I know why I’m not in Forbes. However, I had the same question regarding other traders (not necessarily in RTM) who shows their amazing consistent winners and makes you think that it’s so easy, and the issue that they don’t explain the reasons behind their entries and they never show you the bad trades. Some might learn something from their charts and others might not but in the end they could mislead you into a disaster. The internet is a double edged sword, anyone can post anything wether its false charts or demo results. Here in RTM we tried to avoid this by digging deep into the price action and teaching the reasons behind our entries and what is expected next, and we wish from whoever learns from our posts to carry on the torch and pass it over to new members. So if you want my advice whatever results you see on the internet by others doesn’t mean anything, all you need is a method or to learn how to read the price and your results depends on you and your circumstances, if someone is showing unbelievable results it’s not necessarily fake or even true, and it doesn’t mean that you could achieve the same because by end of the day it all comes back to a certain factors. 1) Your personality & your circumstances: in short you can call it “psychology”, human behavior is common and the proof is if you meet a group of traders and share your trading stories you’ll find that you all share a lot in common, the same mistakes, fears, greed etc. and only the elite who succeed in this business are the ones who succeed in controlling their feelings and bend the circumstances to their advantage. Each trader has different circumstances that could either help or add pressure, for example are you trading part time or full time and do you have enough savings or do you need every cent you can make from the market, does your family/GF/BF supports you or not and so on. 2) Your startup capital: the amount of money in your balance plays a big role, the bigger accounts the better, of course rules are made to be broken and a trader can make thousands out of a hundred but also there are traders who wasted millions. 3) Your money/risk management: this one is much related to 1 & 2 as a cause & effect. MM could vary depending on capital and the personality, bigger capital allows you to use bigger contracts and bad MM could demolish big accounts, solid MM ease psychological pressure and bad psychology destroy all MM rules, the examples are endless. 4) Market state or condition: in some market conditions you can make huge profits, in others you can’t make a clear decision. For example if we consider you’re implementing a solid MM plan trading one instrument and everything is perfect the monthly ROI will never ever have the same result for the 12 month. In fact all the above mentioned points are related and connected to each other as affects & effects. So back to the Forbes list, I’ve never posted consistent profitable trades neither here nor anywhere else and never claimed that I achieve a certain monthly ROI, so this question is irrelevant to me but it could be asked to others if they claim or show such results. As for the yearly return % in general it depends on all the above points. In some of the live competitions traders have achieved more than a 100% ROI in one month, as for institutions, banks, hedge funds, regulated managed accounts etc their risk profiles are monitored & controlled by regulations so as their returns. Moreover, not all private independent traders show off or publish their ROI. Therefore, if you find anyone on the internet or anywhere else publishing their results without teaching then either they are scammers or showing off or looking for investors or selling something. Here in RTM we had an objective to teach and maybe someday become a physical academy or even startup a fund management, this is why most of our posts are showing the why and how before or after and not just after the facts results. So in short take the trading methods as it is or tweak it and ignore others results, don’t think about it, in the end your returns depends on YOU. I don’t guarantee that you’ll make a 100% yearly ROI or less or more because I don’t know you or your circumstances but I guarantee that you’ll save a lot of time in your journey. Cheers, Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Reason: I missed the QM Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 14 Sep 2013 07:20 #321 Hotochin --> Offline Trader Posts: 588 Thank you received: 729 The first is the daily chart. The only main point here is to see the very important level that price was coming into. Price generally either flagged above or below this line. The second chart is the 30min. Basically you have price come into the green line and FO by the pin bar. As Pedini has said the FO really fuels the move in the opposite direction. After the FO price engulfs the green ellipse and gives clues that it wants to go up farther. Your entry is by the return to the source of the engulf marked with a purple line. The last chart is your 1min. I would bet that you went long when price returned to the green rectangle as price had already gone up and taken out supply. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 19 Dec 2013 21:26 #335 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Founders and members of RTM, I’ve been in this market since 2003 and during all these years I’ve never been an active forum member or blogger, until the PIE thread back at FF has changed that for some time all the way to AG until our journals were taken from us then Ifmyante gathered our group and we all helped in founding RTM. The idea of RTM was to create a global home for traders who share the same trading style based on a pure price action and we all have shared the same dream of developing this community to become a free school for new traders and in addition to a fee service to the elite or whoever want a special mentoring, as well as to start up a fund management service by creating our own capital corporation as you can see in the ladder page where I myself have created the organizational structure slides. We all had great ideas and each of us was unique with his/her own unique expertise and skills. However, speaking for myself the first few months spent in founding RTM and establishing the foundation of a trading group has caused me too much as a waste of time on something that requires a non-trader to work on. Since the end of 2011 and I’ve been a full-time trader and my life was great before RTM till it flipped over. Let me be clear here, I’m not blaming RTM for anything, actually I blame none but myself. However, I’ve learned a great lesson from that period of time and a true serious trader who depends a lot on his trading income shouldn’t be able to spend time on chatting, blogging, online communities, etc. unless in a certain special cases if the trader has a spare time, or has another source of income, or seeking a new source of income. Everyone in the cyber space has his/her own agenda and when agendas are gathered together they either meet or collide. My own agenda is to be the best I can be as a trader in regards to knowledge and experience (this has already been confirmed to me personally by the testimonial of an institutional trader and a market maker), and to make my first million out of trading which I failed to do this year. All I desire is to live on my profits from trading as much as I can squeeze out of it, no need to manage other’s money or to offer a commercial service because if I do so then I’m not the TRADER I’m seeking to be, I could be anything else like an analyst or a teacher or a scammer but not a TRADER. With my skills and a decent capital I can achieve what I desire so why should I waste time and efforts on anything else when I can spend it on family, friends, by the pool, or whatever!!! However, as mentioned before if I have the spare time and it’s not going to affect me in any bad way or cause me trouble then maybe I would give sometime to blogging or whatever even though that I’ve learned one should keep his knowledge to himself, but depending on a human nature some are too generous or like to give something back. My advice from an anonymous to every serious trader and it’s up to you to take it or leave it, seek the knowledge anywhere you can find it but don’t waste your time on anything more if you want to succeed as a trader. Trading requires a lot of mental awareness and sensibility so don’t let anything or anyone else disturb it. Stop wasting your time on child’s play and pay attention to the chart or read macro-economic or intermarket analysis or anything that will help you to develop and reach your extremes. Start reading between the lines and stop being spoon-fed. Don’t chase everything that is glowing in the dark or you could end up in a deep hole. Stop arguing about nonsense and let it be, take what works for you and create your own style, don’t mimic others and don’t let them affect you with their own styles, be unique and digest whatever you can, don’t try to absorb everything one-shot, take your time and step by step. Having said that, I’ve decided to remove myself from any association with any website/group which I don’t solely own or have a full control over, including RTM and by doing so I’ve published my own articles in my own blog, and I don’t know if I will ever post over there or do any new activities but for the time being my work should be in the right place where it belongs. Therefore, I ‘m requesting RTM to remove my articles from the markepedia and any other work I’ve done for RTM except for this journal which I expect to remain here for the old time’s sake. This shall be my last post here and any other public forum. Please don’t post any questions or send me any private messages which I’m not going to answer. Oh yeah, and I’m not asking anyone to follow me because I have nothing to offer and have no secret agenda. RTM admins, founders and members I wish you all the best and many thanks to all of you and please forgive me for my faults and mistakes. May you find peace. FAREWELL. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. The following user(s) said Thank You: extremerrage , cuimingzhao Sniffing Snake Tracks... Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 08 Nov 2012 17:30 #83 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 I've posted this in another topic and thought its time to bring it here. Supply and Demand zones presents a stack of unfilled orders either offers or bids. Stacked zones presents stacked groups of unfilled orders (limit orders), in every zone there are unfilled stacked orders on top of each other. As Sam Seiden explained in a webinar - Unfilled orders moves market prices, the chart line/candles/bars represent filled/executed orders and the white space around it represent the unfilled orders (limit orders) thus we need to focus on the big unfilled orders (limit orders). What I'll explain here based on my understanding of the market, so is the attached chart. The following example applies to the stacked zones as well as to stacked orders inside a zone. In case of a supply zone scenario, offers shall be stacked above each other (the wider the zone the more orders at different prices) logically the majority would sell high and this is why unfilled orders would increase higher into the zone, and with every return of price to the zone the sell orders are decrease/consumed.....now this doesn't mean the smart or big money would be sitting at the highest prices....nope not necessarily....they are the smart money and they know where to set and distribute their offers...well, it could be among the highest offers and "for example" this is where a compression ends, or it might be among the lowest offers and this is when we see a spike or a quick touch of the zone's lower edge then price bounce and drops for a long distant before returning back and starts pressuring or forming ascending triangle to consumes the entire zone. Of course the strength of buyers and sellers plays a big role in this process every time it happens, thus comes the importance of how did the price leave the zone, and how is price returning to the zone. I hope I made it simple and clear. P.S. The attached chart present Buy/Sell limit orders. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. I don't know how the pros play it but for me its a sign that i respect on HTF and would enter the entrancement on LTF if its in confluence with levels. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 10 Jan 2013 21:15 #138 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Before I post again about the LQ gap I decided to give a brief about the mechanism of order flow. When we look at the market watch window in our trading platform we'll see instrument's symbols and next to it two different prices, one is bid and another is ask or offer. These prices are offered to us through an online broker who is not just a broker but also a dealer. Here is an explanation of the difference between a dealer and a broker from “Trading & Exchanges – By Larry Harris” “Dealers and brokers help people trade. Dealers trade with their clients when their clients want to trade. The prices at which a dealer will buy and sell are the dealer's bid and ask prices. After they trade with their clients, dealers then try to trade out at a profit by selling what they have bought or by buying back what they have sold. In effect, clients pay dealers to take their trading problems. The dealers then try to solve them at a profit. Dealers profit by buying low and selling high. Successful dealers must be excellent traders. Brokers are agents who arrange trades for their clients. They help their clients find traders who are willing to trade with them. They profit by charging commissions.” Dealers are market makers and also known as market specialists, so they play a big role as a market participant beside other pro money, understand how they play and you'll play with them. Now we understand that online brokers act as brokers and dealers. Back to the market watch window, the ask/offer shows the lowest offer price which the market is willing to sell at and for you to buy, the bid shows the highest bid price which the market is willing to buy at and for you to sell. I’ve already explained in posts # 82 & 83 that there are two types of orders “market orders” and “limit orders”, in the below image I’m showing an example of an order book aka; DOM, Price Ladder, Level II. Bear in mind this is only for explanation purposes, in the currency and metals market there is no depth of market, it only applies to centralized markets such as futures, equities and commodities. Of course banks and dealers have their own order books but it only shows their clients orders. Back to the image, the offers column for sell limit orders, bids for buy limit orders and in middle is the price. I’ve taken eur/usd prices as an example, the orders are standard lots/contracts (i.e. 200K = 2 lots and 1M = 10 lots), the two prices highlighted in yellow presents the Bid/Ask prices which we see on our market watch (spread in this example is unrealistic and only to make it easier). Anyone entering the market with market orders will be buying at 1.3010 and selling at 1.300. Now let’s say you place buy market orders of 500K (5 lots) what will happen?! Your orders will be executed at the lowest offer consuming the 200K at 1.3010 and a 300K out of 500K at 1.3020 and this price will be the new lowest offer with 200K limit orders, so as offers were consumed price moved up to 1.3020 Another scenario; you are placing a sell market orders of 1 million (10 lots) what is the outcome?! Your orders will be executed at the highest bid consuming the 400K at 1.3000, the 200K at 1.2980, the 300K at 1.2970 and a 100K out of 500K at 1.2950 and this will be the new highest bid with 400K limit orders, so as bids were consumed price moved down to 1.2950 This is how price moves up or down by consuming orders, and bigger orders generate buying/selling pressure (momentum) pushing price in its direction consuming smaller orders until it stops (slow down /stalls) at a new price where there are opposite bigger orders are placed. One last thing, the stop loss pending orders considered as limit orders and when it’s executed it adds pressure in the opposite direction. Hope it makes sense. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 10 Jan 2013 21:47 #139 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 benhur wrote: I hope it is alright to ask you to elaborate more about "Liquidity Gaps"? What is your definition for it, and how (and why) you expect of price reaction at this zone? There is no easy way to answer this question as it requires a study of the market microstructure which explains how the exchange occurs in financial markets and the order flow process, but I’ll try my best to make it simple. First of all, kindly refer to my first post here for some of the terms which in my opinion share the same meaning/definition. Second, take a look at my previous post. Now back to basics, we know that in a normal market there is a buyer for every seller and vice versa, this is considered a stable state of the market as it creates a balance between supply and demand and maintains a stable price value, and as the imbalance occurs in supply and demand the value of price increases or decreases. The balance state on chart is presented by a consolidation in price, and the imbalance is presented by a high volume/momentum candle (also known as a sign of Pro money / Institution’s buying or selling). If we zoom into the consolidation (LTF) we’ll find other states of balance and imbalance. In post # 90 I tried to explain the orders execution of Pro money but not in an accurate way for the sake of simplicity but I fixed this in post # 138 . So to recap, to have your orders executed at current price you need someone on the opposite side with the same number of orders otherwise price will move too much from your entry until it finds an opposite stack of orders. Retail traders have a big advantage over Pro money since their orders gets executed at current price, a benefit that Pro money doesn't have due to their mass orders which gets executed at different prices as the price moves away from their initial entry. Hence they tend to accumulate/distribute their orders through positioning, re-entering on retracements or even creating them as well as fakeouts. By now you understand why the sign of pro money buying/selling is presented by a high momentum candles aka liquidity gaps, but if you didn’t then continue reading. A liquidity gap means low liquidity! How?! You know that for every seller there is buyer and vice versa, but you also know that online brokers doesn't guarantee a non-slippage or a no requote during the time of volatile news due to low liquidity, and you can clearly see it on charts during such news when a long spike or a high momentum candle is created in seconds. A broker wouldn’t take the risk if you’re buying because they can't find you a seller unless they are willing to take the risk by selling to you as a dealer. So how could we say it’s a low liquidity when price moves in a high volume?! Again, we know that high volume candles are signs of big money, institutions etc. entering the market or in control of the market pushing price in their direction...that is true. However, the liquidity in this case is being injected from one side only (the dominant side) hence the name liquidity gap and the equation “Volatility = High Volume + Low Liquidity”. Now bear with me...The "normal or balanced state" of the market is to have a seller for every buyer thus maintaining an efficient change in price value, so when the market moves up or down either in consolidation or in a ladder steps behavior then it’s a “normal balanced state”, but if it moves up or down with a high volatility and momentum then its "abnormal or imbalanced state" and it’s a sign of domination from one side. So what is the conclusion?! The dominant side (pro money) would want to collect their profits as soon as price reaches their targets which results in market returning to its previous state (rebalancing) on a wider scale and filling up the liquidity gaps. I've answered the definition part in your question; now let’s answer the other part related to price reaction. I hope you were paying attention to the Fiber and Cable as I suggested in post # 134 and you can see the marked LQ gaps in the process of getting filled. Look at the attached Gold daily chart and see the price behavior when it broke through a decision area/major key level of significance, creating a high momentum candle as a sign of one side in control bulls or bears and how they wanted to push it higher or lower, of course price will return to fill the LQ gaps but later in the future, not at the present time of the incident specially not when it starts creating a new DP/Supply/Demand as pro money position their entries. On the other hand you can notice larger high momentum candles (LQ gaps) gets filled the same day or next day but this happens when price fails to break through a major DP or key level and this is when pro money are accumulating/distributing (bottom and top side of the chart). The market is in a constant state/phase shifting from normal to abnormal, balanced to imbalanced and back again, just the same as in ranging to trending and back again (refer to Wyckoff Market Theory), and every range contains the two phases if you zoom in, the same applies to the bottom consolidated area in chart. Supply & Demand trading style is in harmony with order flow and it works according to this stuff. IMO if we learn how the market works from the beginning it would save us a lot of time in our journey. Hope I made it clear and simple. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 16 Jan 2013 23:12 #153 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 MCFly wrote: I'm not sure I like the explaination in the vids about why the gaps tend to get closed but in the end they mostly do. Everyone points at S/D, Balance and Imbalance in a different way without explaining the real mechanics behind it. Although in my previous posts I tried to make it clear why LQ gap gets filled but I’ll repeat it in a simple phrase; In LTF LQ gaps gets filled because - When pro money enter with their big orders it moves the price away and against their entry price, therefore they await for price to return to their entry price to continue adding their orders. In HTF LQ gaps get filled because – When pro money closes their buy orders they become sellers, and when they close their sell orders they become buyers. Thank you. Next is H4 TF, and potential zones and S/R flips from the same TF. on the far right side price broke through the S/R and into the DBD which has a stacked DP from LTF, a successful retest of the broken S/R will push the price higher to the KL and lower edge of the large broken demand (now supply) where it should drop from there. Finally M30 TF, same levels from previous TF and a clearer view of the stacked supply zones. So now it's up to PA to guide us. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Tony Robbins Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Sniffing Price 05 Feb 2013 00:50 #183 FX.Sniffer Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 580 Thank you received: 1547 Reading COT (Commitment of Traders) charts to identify the long term market sentiment; COT charts are based on COT reports which are published by the CFTC each Friday on 19:30 GMT. From outsource: There are several reasons why COT charts provide valuable information about changes in the position commitments of traders: - Flips in the overall positioning may be an accurate trending indicator - Extreme positioning has historically been accurate in identifying FX market reversals - Changes in overall number of contracts can be used to determine strength of trend The below chart shows the recent Commercial Traders Net (Hedgers, Exporters, Importers), Large Speculators Net (hedge funds, banks etc) and Small Traders Net. What concern me are the Commercial and the Speculators, and the index values of 100 and Zero on the vertical axis. Knowing that when one is at 100 the other will be at Zero and vice versa. So, let’s take a look at the euro COT chart and compare it with eur/usd chart. Starting with old history from 5 Jun 2012, Com traders index was at 100 and Spec index at 0 and eur futures price 1.2446 (marked by a vertical dotted line on COT chart). Present time on 29 Jan 2013, Com traders index at 0 and Spec traders index at 100, and eur futures price 1.3665 In below chart prices are marked by horizontal lines and dates marked by vertical lines. The first lower crossing on the left side at [1] represents the old history and the second higher crossing on the right side at [2] represents the present. The weekly chart for an overall view and daily is for a zoom in view. Now, let’s start the analysis…..at [1] the Com were very long and Spec were very short, so what happened in price? It moved up above support level and into a decision area of supply followed by a move down to the 1.2446 value and bounced up retesting S/R level then back down into demand area from weekly TF where the stacked buy orders are, and price reversed in direction to rally up. Now open your eyes wide open and look at the level 1.2446 and see how it acted as an S/R flip level. So why price didn’t reverse directly from 1.2446?? Simply because of positioning. At [2] present time the Com are very short and Spec are very long, so what will happen? Currently we can see price bouncing down from 1.3665 to S/R level and two stacked decision areas of demand, but where are the stack sell orders on chart??! Yes at the higher decision area of stacked supply. So let’s see how it goes in the coming days. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic.
